(x=47 y=60) width=266.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=WXOXOD+CMR8
Abstract. In the 1980s Branner and Douady discovered a surgery relating
(x=37 y=87) width=286.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=PDEGEZ+CMCSC10
various limbs of the Mandelbrot set.
(x=37 y=97) width=137.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=WXOXOD+CMR8
We put this surgery in the framework
(x=180 y=97) width=143.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=WXOXOD+CMR8
of “Pacman Renormalization Theory” that combines features of quadratic-like
(x=37 y=107) width=286.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=WXOXOD+CMR8
and Siegel renormalizations.
(x=37 y=117) width=106.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=WXOXOD+CMR8
We show that Siegel renormalization periodic
(x=150 y=117) width=173.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=WXOXOD+CMR8
points (constructed by McMullen in the 1990s) can be promoted to pacman
(x=37 y=127) width=286.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=WXOXOD+CMR8
renormalization periodic points.
(x=37 y=137) width=120.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=WXOXOD+CMR8
Then we prove that these periodic points
(x=164 y=137) width=159.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=WXOXOD+CMR8
are hyperbolic with one-dimensional unstable manifold resolving a long stand-
(x=37 y=147) width=286.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=WXOXOD+CMR8
ing problem.
(x=37 y=157) width=47.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=WXOXOD+CMR8
As a consequence, we obtain the scaling laws for the centers
(x=91 y=157) width=232.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=WXOXOD+CMR8
of satellite components of the Mandelbrot set near the corresponding Siegel
(x=37 y=167) width=286.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=WXOXOD+CMR8
parameters.
(x=37 y=177) width=43.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=WXOXOD+CMR8

1. Introduction
(x=140 y=427) width=80.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
Renormalization was introduced into Dynamics in the mid 1970s by Feigenbaum,
(x=13 y=445) width=346.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
Coullet and Tresser and since then has established itself as a powerful tool of pen-
(x=1 y=457) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
etrating into the small-scale structure of phase portraits and bifurcation loci. It
(x=1 y=469) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
turned out to be challenging to develop a rigorous mathematical theory of renor-
(x=1 y=481) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
malization (for example, to prove hyperbolicity of the renormalization operator),
(x=1 y=493) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
but every time when this is achieved, plentiful deep consequences reward the e?ort.
(x=1 y=505) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
The complex quadratic family provided us with several important renormaliza-
(x=13 y=517) width=346.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
tion schemes: quadratic-like, near-parabolic, and Siegel. All three are intimately
(x=1 y=529) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
related to the observed self-similarity of the Mandelbrot setM and to the celebrated
(x=1 y=539) width=358.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
1
(x=178 y=554) width=4.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=GTIZRH+CMR7

(x=37 y=269) width=286.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=PDEGEZ+CMCSC10

(x=37 y=283) width=221.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
MLC Conjecture on the local connectivity of M. The conjecture comes in two ?a-
(x=1 y=316) width=358.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
vors, “primitive” and “satellite”. Development of the quadratic-like renormaliza-
(x=1 y=329) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
tion has led to a substantial progress in the primitive case, while the near-parabolic
(x=1 y=341) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
renormalization has given an insight into the satellite situation.
(x=1 y=353) width=277.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
In this paper we design a new “pacman” renormalization and prove the hyper-
(x=13 y=365) width=346.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
bolicity of the corresponding renormalization operator. It implies the hyperbolicity
(x=1 y=377) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
of the Siegel renormalization for arbitrary periodic combinatorics (resolving a prob-
(x=1 y=389) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
lem going back to the early 1980s) and gives an insight into the self-similarity of
(x=1 y=401) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
the Mandelbrot set near the main cardioid. In the 2nd part of this project [DL] we
(x=1 y=413) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
will give further applications by proving local connectivity of the Mandelbrot set at
(x=1 y=425) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
some satellite parameters of bounded type (that had been previously out of reach)
(x=1 y=437) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
and showing that the corresponding Julia sets have positive area.
(x=1 y=449) width=285.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
1.1. Statements of the results. Though the Mandelbrot set M is highly non-
(x=1 y=472) width=358.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
homogeneous, it possesses some remarkable self-similarity features. Most notable is
(x=1 y=486) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
the presence of baby Mandelbrot sets inside M which are almost indistinguishable
(x=1 y=496) width=358.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
fromM itself. The explanation of this phenomenon is provided by the Renormaliza-
(x=1 y=508) width=358.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
tion Theory for quadratic-like maps, which has been a central theme in Holomorphic
(x=1 y=522) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
Dynamics since the mid-1980s (see [DH2,S,McM1, L1] and references therein).
(x=1 y=534) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
By exploring the pictures, one can also observe that the Mandelbrot set has self-
(x=13 y=546) width=346.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
similarity features near its main cardioid. For instance, as Figure 2 indicates, near
(x=1 y=558) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
the (anti-)golde?n mean point, the (pn/pn+2)-limbs of M scale down at rate ??2n,
(x=1 y=566) width=358.000000 height=13.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
where ? = (1 +
(x=1 y=583) width=66.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
5)/2 and pn are the Fibonacci numbers. The goal of this paper is
(x=77 y=583) width=282.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
to develop a renormalization theory responsible for this phenomenon.

(x=13 y=158) width=346.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10

(x=1 y=170) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10

(x=1 y=206) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10

(x=1 y=218) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10

1.3. More historical comments. Renormalization of Siegel maps appeared ?rst
(x=1 y=392) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
in the work by physicists (see [Wi,MN,MP]) as a mechanism for self-similarity of
(x=1 y=404) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
the golden mean Siegel disk near the critical point. A few years later, Douady and
(x=1 y=416) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
Ghys discovered a surgery that reduces previously unaccessible geometric problems
(x=1 y=428) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
for Siegel disks1 of bounded type to much better understood problems for critical
(x=1 y=437) width=358.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
circle maps. This led, in particular, to the local connectivity result for Siegel Julia
(x=1 y=452) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
sets of bounded type (Petersen [Pe]) and also became a key to the mathematical
(x=1 y=464) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
study of the Siegel renormalization.
(x=1 y=476) width=159.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
In particular, McMullen-Yampolsky theory
(x=168 y=476) width=191.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
mentioned above (see §1.2) is based upon this machinery.
(x=1 y=486) width=250.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
Holomorphic commuting pairs (as well as almost commuting holomorphic pairs)
(x=13 y=500) width=346.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
were studied by Stirnemann. In [St], he gave a computer-assisted proof of the exis-
(x=1 y=512) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
tence of a renormalization ?xed point with a golden-mean Siegel disk and showed
(x=1 y=523) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
that the quadratic polynomial with a golden-mean Siegel disk converges to that
(x=1 y=535) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
?xed point. Recently, Gaidashev and Yampolsky gave a computer-assisted proof of
(x=1 y=547) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
the hyperbolicity of the renormalization for the golden mean rotation number [GY].
(x=1 y=559) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
1The original surgery applies to Siegel polynomials only. Its extension to general Siegel maps
(x=13 y=583) width=346.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=WXOXOD+CMR8
leads to quasicritical circle maps, see [AL2].

(x=1 y=180) width=358.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10

(x=1 y=192) width=358.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
shrinks to x(g); i.e. R(g) lands at x(g).

(x=1 y=285) width=165.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
Conjecture C.2 and its generalizations describe in a convenient way how an
(x=13 y=297) width=346.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
attracting ?xed point bifurcates into repelling. An important consequence is control
(x=1 y=309) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
of the post-critical set: if a perturbation of fc is within Mol, then the new post-
(x=1 y=319) width=358.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
critical set is within a small neighborhood of Hc. A statement of this sort (for
(x=1 y=333) width=358.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
parabolic parameters approximating a Siegel polynomial) was proven by Bu? and
(x=1 y=345) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
Che?ritat, see [BC, Corollary 4]. This was a necessary ingredient in constructing a
(x=1 y=357) width=358.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LXWHWS+CMR10
Julia set with positive measure.
